### Task 9: The language server — rsdl diagnostics, hover and go-to-definition

**Model:** Opus. **Story:** E6.15. **Spec:** rsdl reference §3.1 (RSDL-602),
§3.2 (a `requires` line naming an inline-shape service), §4 (references read by
case), §5 (RSDL-804), §7 (instances), §12 (posture). Plan decision P-B4.

This task makes the language server read `.rsdl` files as the command line does:
an opened or edited `.rsdl` file shows what `check_system` raises and the
RSDL-804 warnings `ridl check` shows, and hover and go-to-definition work on
every rsdl reference. It also rewrites the service hover's posture note to rsdl
v0.2.

**Decisions in this task.**

- **The server runs `check_system` once per analysis, over the loaded
  workspace.** `ServerState::analyze` today runs `parse_file`, `resolve_package`
  and `check_package` per package, including the packages' `.rsdl` files (Task
  1), but not the system query. After the package loop it now runs
  `check_system` and remaps its diagnostics with the workspace's render ids in
  package-then-file order, as `ridlc::load_and_check` does. A `.rsdl` file
  opened from outside the loaded workspace (a standalone overlay) is outside the
  closure (rsdl §3.1) and gets its parse diagnostics only.
- **The server raises RSDL-804 with the empty claimed set.** rsdl §5 raises
  RSDL-804 for a namespace "no configured backend claims". The language server
  configures no backend, as the command line does not (P-B4), so the same
  warnings appear in the editor and in `ridl check`, and a later configured set
  reaches both drivers through one parameter. The alternative, no RSDL-804 in
  the editor, would make the editor show fewer findings than `ridl check` for
  the same file; the warning is a warning, so it never marks a line as an error.
  The builder moves from `ridlc` to `ridl_sem::unclaimed_backend_keys`, beside
  the model it reads: `ridl-lsp` does not depend on `ridlc` (that would link
  every backend into the server), and the message must not be written twice. The
  corrections section updates P-B4's text.
- **The target comes from the checker, not from a second resolver.**
  `ridl_sem::rsdl::reference_at` finds the model `Reference` whose site holds
  the cursor. An `offers` or `requires` line reads the component's resolved line
  in `CheckedSystem::component_lines` (a service name, an `InterfaceId`); a
  member line of a `system`, `distribution` or `machine`, and a `for` reference,
  go through `closure::Lookup::{component, system}` and the service catalog, the
  lookups the checker binds those references with, under the checker's slot
  rules: `Name.inst` only in a `machine` line (RSDL-602 and RSDL-903 elsewhere),
  and a service as a member line only when no declared component offers it
  (RSDL-504). A reference the checker binds to nothing (it drew an error) has no
  target, so no hover and no definition. The syntax tree is read for one thing
  only: which segment of `Name.inst` the cursor is on. The function lives in
  `ridl-sem` because `Lookup` is private to the rsdl module.
- **Where each target leads, and what its hover shows.**

  | Reference                                        | Definition                            | Hover                                                                    |
  | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
  | a component (member line, `Name` of `Name.inst`) | the name in `component Name`          | package-qualified name, instances or the unit instance, lines as written |
  | an instance segment                              | the name in the `instances` list      | `pkg.Name.inst` and the rsdl §7 sentence                                 |
  | the `for` reference                              | the name in `system Name`             | package-qualified name and member lines                                  |
  | a service (`offers`, member line)                | the dotted name in the ridl `service` | the ridl service hover, posture note included                            |
  | an interface on a `requires` line                | the name in the ridl `interface`      | the ridl interface hover                                                 |
  | an inline-shape service on a `requires` line     | the dotted name in the ridl `service` | the ridl service hover                                                   |

- **An `.rsdl` file never reaches the typl hover or navigation.** The server
  dispatches on `profile_of_path`: in an `.rsdl` file `nav::symbol_at` would
  read a component name as a type reference, which names nothing or a typl
  declaration of the same name.
- **Out of scope, debt to file.** Find-references, rename, completion and inlay
  hints in an `.rsdl` file keep their typl behaviour (E6.15 names diagnostics,
  hover and navigation): the executor files one issue for rsdl-aware references,
  rename and completion. The server also publishes no `service_catalog`
  diagnostic (RIDL-140), which `ridlc` merges beside the rsdl ones; that gap
  predates this plan, and the executor files it as a second issue.
- **The posture note.** `POSTURE_NOTE` in `crates/ridl-lsp/src/hover.rs` states
  rsdl v0.1 ("rsdl and deployment choose the posture"); rsdl v0.2 §12 derives no
  posture and reserves the derivation. Step 6 quotes the old and the new text.
  The new text follows the ridl reference §14.5 bullet "Posture-neutral by
  design", which the plan's own pull request (B2) already changed to mark the
  derivation deferred; this task does not edit the ridl reference.

**Shared files.** `crates/ridlc/src/lib.rs` (S1 → C4 Task 7 → #324 → B4) and
`crates/ridlc/tests/corpus.rs` (not in lanes plan §6; L4 also changes it). Step
1 checks both.

**Files** (line numbers are after Task 8):

- Create: `crates/ridl-sem/src/rsdl/target.rs`, `crates/ridl-lsp/src/rsdl.rs`
- Modify: `crates/ridl-sem/src/rsdl/mod.rs` (the module list, line 22; the
  re-exports and `use`, lines 29-31; before the doc comment of `Site`, line

```rust
// --- the rsdl system layer (E6.15) ----------------------------------------

/// The contracts the rsdl fixture's system is built from: two interfaces, a
/// named-shape service for each, and an inline-shape service.
const RSDL_CONTRACTS: &str = "package veh.adas\n\
\n\
type Flag: boolean\n\
\n\
interface CruiseControl {\n\
\x20 signal engaged: Flag @[100ms..1s]\n\
}\n\
\n\
interface LaneAssist {\n\
\x20 signal active: Flag @[100ms..1s]\n\
}\n\
\n\
service veh.adas.cruise : CruiseControl\n\
\n\
service veh.adas.lane : LaneAssist\n\
\n\
service veh.adas.access {\n\
\x20 signal alive: Flag @[1s..10s]\n\
}\n";

/// The rsdl fixture: each reference form of rsdl reference §4 in a slot that
/// admits it — a component in a system and a distribution, an instance, a bare
/// and a qualified interface, an inline-shape service after `requires`, a lone
/// service, and a `for` reference. It checks with one warning, RSDL-804 on
/// `linux.cpuset`.
const RSDL_SYSTEM: &str = "package veh.topology\n\
\n\
import veh.adas.LaneAssist\n\
\n\
component Cruise [ instances = (primary, backup) ] {\n\
\x20 offers veh.adas.cruise\n\
\x20 requires LaneAssist\n\
}\n\
\n\
component Lane { offers veh.adas.lane }\n\
\n\
component Panel { requires veh.adas.LaneAssist, requires veh.adas.access }\n\
\n\
system Vehicle { Cruise, Lane, Panel, veh.adas.access }\n\
\n\
distribution Adas { veh.adas.access, Cruise, Lane, Panel }\n\
\n\
deployment Production for Vehicle {\n\
\x20 machine Hpc { Cruise.primary, Lane, veh.adas.access }\n\
\x20 machine Cockpit { Cruise.backup, Panel [ linux.cpuset = (2, 3) ] }\n\
}\n";

/// Writes the rsdl fixture as a two-member workspace and returns the
/// `(contracts, system)` file URIs.
fn write_rsdl_workspace(dir: &TempDir) -> (lt::Uri, lt::Uri) {
    dir.write(
        "ridl.toml",
        "[workspace]\nmembers = [\"adas\", \"topology\"]\n",
    );
    for (member, package) in [("adas", "veh.adas"), ("topology", "veh.topology")] {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.path().join(member)).expect("create the member directory");
        dir.write(
            &format!("{member}/ridl.toml"),
            &format!("[package]\nname = \"{package}\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\n"),
        );
    }
    let contracts = dir.write("adas/adas.ridl", RSDL_CONTRACTS);
    let system = dir.write("topology/system.rsdl", RSDL_SYSTEM);
    (uri_of(&contracts), uri_of(&system))
}

/// The range of the `occurrence`-th match of `needle` in `text`, on one line.
fn range_of(text: &str, needle: &str, occurrence: usize) -> lt::Range {
    let start = find_pos(text, needle, occurrence);
    let length = needle.encode_utf16().count() as u32;
    range(
        (start.line, start.character),
        (start.line, start.character + length),
    )
}

/// The position `offset` UTF-16 units into the `occurrence`-th match of
/// `needle` in `text`.
fn pos_in(text: &str, needle: &str, occurrence: usize, offset: u32) -> lt::Position {
    let start = find_pos(text, needle, occurrence);
    pos(start.line, start.character + offset)
}

/// An `.rsdl` file publishes what the rsdl system query raises, and the
/// RSDL-804 warning `ridl check` shows for a backend key no backend claims:
/// from the loaded workspace, then from an edited buffer.
#[test]
fn an_rsdl_file_publishes_the_system_checks_and_rsdl_804() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("rsdl-diagnostics");
    let (_contracts, system) = write_rsdl_workspace(&dir);
    let (client, server) = start(uri_of(dir.path()));

    let initial = next_publish(&client, &system);
    assert_eq!(codes(&initial.diagnostics), vec!["RSDL-804"]);
    assert_eq!(
        initial.diagnostics[0].range,
        range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "linux.cpuset = (2, 3)", 0),
        "the warning spans the whole backend key",
    );
    assert_eq!(
        initial.diagnostics[0].severity,
        Some(lt::DiagnosticSeverity::WARNING)
    );

    // A system member line naming nothing is RSDL-602 (rsdl reference §3.1),
    // which only the system query raises.
    let edited = RSDL_SYSTEM.replace(
        "Panel, veh.adas.access }",
        "Panel, veh.adas.access, Missing }",
    );
    did_open(&client, &system, &edited);
    let opened = next_publish(&client, &system);
    assert_eq!(codes(&opened.diagnostics), vec!["RSDL-602", "RSDL-804"]);
    assert_eq!(opened.diagnostics[0].range, range_of(&edited, "Missing", 0));
    assert_eq!(
        opened.diagnostics[0].severity,
        Some(lt::DiagnosticSeverity::ERROR)
    );

    shut_down(&client, 2);
    server.join().expect("thread joins").expect("clean exit");
}

/// Go-to-definition follows each rsdl reference form (rsdl reference §4) to
/// the declaration the checker binds it to: in the `.rsdl` file for a
/// component, an instance and the system, in the ridl contract for a service
/// and an interface.
#[test]
fn goto_definition_follows_each_rsdl_reference_form() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("rsdl-goto");
    let (contracts, system) = write_rsdl_workspace(&dir);
    let (client, server) = start(uri_of(dir.path()));

    // (what the cursor is on, cursor, file and range of the declaration).
    let cases = [
        (
            "a component in a system member line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "{ Cruise, Lane", 0, 3),
            &system,
            range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise", 0),
        ),
        (
            "a component in a distribution member line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "access, Cruise", 0, 9),
            &system,
            range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise", 0),
        ),
        (
            "the instance segment of a placement line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise.primary", 0, 9),
            &system,
            range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "primary", 0),
        ),
        (
            "the component segment of a placement line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise.backup", 0, 2),
            &system,
            range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise", 0),
        ),
        (
            "the `for` reference",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "for Vehicle", 0, 5),
            &system,
            range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Vehicle", 0),
        ),
        (
            "a service on an `offers` line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "offers veh.adas.cruise", 0, 12),
            &contracts,
            range_of(RSDL_CONTRACTS, "veh.adas.cruise", 0),
        ),
        (
            "a bare interface on a `requires` line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "requires LaneAssist", 0, 10),
            &contracts,
            range_of(RSDL_CONTRACTS, "LaneAssist", 0),
        ),
        (
            "a qualified interface on a `requires` line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "requires veh.adas.LaneAssist", 0, 20),
            &contracts,
            range_of(RSDL_CONTRACTS, "LaneAssist", 0),
        ),
        (
            "an inline-shape service on a `requires` line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "requires veh.adas.access", 0, 12),
            &contracts,
            range_of(RSDL_CONTRACTS, "veh.adas.access", 0),
        ),
        (
            "a lone service in a placement line",
            pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Lane, veh.adas.access }", 0, 8),
            &contracts,
            range_of(RSDL_CONTRACTS, "veh.adas.access", 0),
        ),
    ];
    for (id, (what, cursor, uri, declaration)) in (10..).zip(cases) {
        let response = definition_at(&client, id, system.clone(), cursor)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{what} resolves"));
        let location = match response {
            lt::GotoDefinitionResponse::Scalar(location) => location,
            other => panic!("{what}: expected a single location, got {other:?}"),
        };
        assert_eq!(location.uri.as_str(), uri.as_str(), "{what}: the file");
        assert_eq!(location.range, declaration, "{what}: the declared name");
    }

    shut_down(&client, 30);
    server.join().expect("thread joins").expect("clean exit");
}

/// A reference the checker binds to nothing has no definition, even when its
/// text names a declaration: an instance in a system member line is RSDL-602
/// (rsdl reference §3.1), and a service that a declared component offers,
/// written as a member line, is RSDL-504 (§6).
#[test]
fn goto_definition_follows_no_rsdl_reference_the_checker_rejects() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("rsdl-goto-rejected");
    let (_contracts, system) = write_rsdl_workspace(&dir);
    let (client, server) = start(uri_of(dir.path()));

    let edited = RSDL_SYSTEM.replace(
        "system Vehicle { Cruise, Lane, Panel, veh.adas.access }",
        "system Vehicle { Cruise.primary, Lane, Panel, veh.adas.access, veh.adas.cruise }",
    );
    did_open(&client, &system, &edited);
    let cases = [
        (
            "an instance in a system member line",
            pos_in(&edited, "{ Cruise.primary", 0, 10),
        ),
        (
            "a service its component offers, in a system member line",
            pos_in(&edited, "access, veh.adas.cruise", 0, 12),
        ),
    ];
    for (id, (what, cursor)) in (10..).zip(cases) {
        let response = definition_at(&client, id, system.clone(), cursor);
        assert!(response.is_none(), "{what}: got {response:?}");
    }

    shut_down(&client, 12);
    server.join().expect("thread joins").expect("clean exit");
}

/// Hover on an rsdl reference renders what it names: a component with its
/// instances and lines, an instance, the system, a ridl interface, and a ridl
/// service with its posture note.
#[test]
fn hover_on_an_rsdl_reference_renders_the_named_declaration() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("rsdl-hover");
    let (_contracts, system) = write_rsdl_workspace(&dir);
    let (client, server) = start(uri_of(dir.path()));
    let hover = |id: i32, needle: &str, offset: u32| {
        let cursor = pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, needle, 0, offset);
        hover_markdown(&client, id, system.clone(), cursor)
    };
    // (request id, cursor needle and offset, what the markdown holds).
    let cases: [(i32, &str, u32, &[&str]); 6] = [
        (
            10,
            "{ Cruise, Lane",
            3,
            &[
                "component veh.topology.Cruise",
                "**Instances:** `primary`, `backup`",
                "**Offers:** `veh.adas.cruise`",
                "**Requires:** `LaneAssist`",
            ],
        ),
        (
            11,
            "Lane, Panel",
            1,
            &["**Instances:** the unit instance `Unit`"],
        ),
        (
            12,
            "for Vehicle",
            5,
            &[
                "system veh.topology.Vehicle",
                "**Members:** `Cruise`, `Lane`, `Panel`, `veh.adas.access`",
            ],
        ),
        (
            13,
            "requires veh.adas.LaneAssist",
            20,
            &["`veh.adas.LaneAssist`", "interface"],
        ),
        (
            14,
            "requires veh.adas.access",
            12,
            &["service veh.adas.access { … }", "**Shape:** inline"],
        ),
        (
            15,
            "offers veh.adas.cruise",
            12,
            &[
                "service veh.adas.cruise : CruiseControl",
                "deriving the posture per deployment is reserved (rsdl §12)",
            ],
        ),
    ];
    for (id, needle, offset, parts) in cases {
        let markdown = hover(id, needle, offset);
        for part in parts {
            assert!(
                markdown.contains(part),
                "`{part}` at `{needle}`:\n{markdown}"
            );
        }
    }

    // An instance hover covers the instance segment only.
    let cursor = pos_in(RSDL_SYSTEM, "Cruise.primary", 0, 9);
    let instance = hover_at(&client, 16, system.clone(), cursor).expect("an instance hovers");
    let lt::HoverContents::Markup(markup) = instance.contents else {
        panic!("expected markdown hover");
    };
    assert!(
        markup.value.contains("veh.topology.Cruise.primary"),
        "{}",
        markup.value
    );
    assert_eq!(instance.range, Some(range_of(RSDL_SYSTEM, "primary", 1)));

    shut_down(&client, 17);
    server.join().expect("thread joins").expect("clean exit");
}
```

In `crates/ridl-sem/src/rsdl/mod.rs`, add `mod target;` after `mod resolve;`

Create `crates/ridl-sem/src/rsdl/target.rs`:

```rust
//! What a reference in an `.rsdl` file names (rsdl reference §4), for the
//! language server. The reference is read from the checked model, and its
//! target from the checker's own results: an `offers` or `requires` line from
//! the component's resolved lines, a member line and a `for` reference through
//! the lookups the checker resolves them with. The language server does not
//! resolve a name itself, so it cannot navigate to a declaration the checker
//! would not bind.

use ridl_core::db::InputFile;
use ridl_core::package::{Package, Workspace, service_catalog};
use ridl_syntax::SyntaxKind;
use ridl_syntax::ast::AstNode;
use rowan::{TextRange, TextSize};

use super::closure::{InterfaceId, Lookup};
use super::{CheckedSystem, Reference, ReferenceForm};
use crate::resolve::source_file;

/// The declaration a reference names.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Target {
    /// A `system`, as an index into [`CheckedSystem::systems`].
    System(usize),
    /// A `component`, as an index into [`CheckedSystem::components`].
    Component(usize),
    /// One declared instance: the component, as an index into
    /// [`CheckedSystem::components`], and the instance, as an index into that
    /// component's `instances` list.
    Instance { component: usize, instance: usize },
    /// A `service`, by its dotted name.
    Service(String),
    /// A declared `interface`, or the inline shape of a service (rsdl §3.2).
    Interface(InterfaceId),
}

/// A reference under a cursor and the declaration it names.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ReferenceTarget {
    /// The reference as the model holds it.
    pub reference: Reference,
    /// The part of the reference that names the target: the instance segment
    /// of `Name.inst` when the cursor is on it, the segments before it when the
    /// cursor is on those, and the whole reference for every other form.
    pub range: TextRange,
    pub target: Target,
}

/// Where a reference is written. Its role depends on the slot (rsdl §4).
enum Slot<'a> {
    /// A member line of a `system`, `distribution` or `machine` in `package`;
    /// `placement` for a machine's, the one member line that may name an
    /// instance (rsdl §4).
    Member { package: &'a str, placement: bool },
    /// The `line`-th `offers` line of the `component`-th component.
    Offers { component: usize, line: usize },
    /// The `line`-th `requires` line of the `component`-th component.
    Requires { component: usize, line: usize },
    /// The `for` reference of a deployment in `package`.
    For { package: &'a str },
}

/// The reference of `system` written at `offset` in `file`, and what it names,
/// or `None` when no reference is there or the checker bound it to nothing.
///
/// `system` is the result of [`check_system`](super::check_system) for `ws`
/// and `std`.
pub fn reference_at(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    system: &CheckedSystem,
    file: InputFile,
    offset: TextSize,
) -> Option<ReferenceTarget> {
    let (reference, slot) = written_references(system)
        .into_iter()
        .find(|(reference, _)| {
            reference.site.file == file && reference.site.range.contains_inclusive(offset)
        })?;
    let reference = reference.clone();
    let whole = reference.site.range;
    let catalog = service_catalog(db, ws, std);
    let lookup = Lookup::new(db, ws, std, system, &catalog);
    let (target, range) = match slot {
        Slot::Offers { component, line } => {
            let service = system.component_lines.get(component)?.offers.get(line)?;
            (Target::Service(service.clone()?), whole)
        }
        Slot::Requires { component, line } => {
            let interface = system.component_lines.get(component)?.requires.get(line)?;
            (Target::Interface(interface.clone()?), whole)
        }
        Slot::For { package } => {
            let ReferenceForm::Declared {
                package: written,
                name,
            } = &reference.form
            else {
                return None;
            };
            let index = lookup.system(package, written.as_deref(), name)?;
            (Target::System(index), whole)
        }
        Slot::Member { package, placement } => {
            match &reference.form {
                ReferenceForm::Declared {
                    package: written,
                    name,
                } => {
                    let index = lookup.component(package, written.as_deref(), name)?;
                    (Target::Component(index), whole)
                }
                // A `system` or `distribution` line naming an instance is
                // RSDL-602 or RSDL-903.
                ReferenceForm::Instance { .. } if !placement => return None,
                ReferenceForm::Instance {
                    package: written,
                    component,
                    instance,
                } => {
                    let index = lookup.component(package, written.as_deref(), component)?;
                    let (component_part, instance_part) = instance_ranges(db, &reference)?;
                    if instance_part.contains_inclusive(offset) {
                        let position = system.components[index]
                            .instances
                            .as_ref()?
                            .iter()
                            .position(|declared| declared.name == *instance)?;
                        let target = Target::Instance {
                            component: index,
                            instance: position,
                        };
                        (target, instance_part)
                    } else {
                        (Target::Component(index), component_part)
                    }
                }
                // A service a declared component offers does not stand for a
                // component (RSDL-504); the checker's `Lookup::record_offers`
                // reads the same resolved `offers` lines.
                ReferenceForm::Service { name }
                    if lookup.catalog.entries.contains_key(name)
                        && !system.component_lines.iter().any(|lines| {
                            lines.offers.iter().flatten().any(|offered| offered == name)
                        }) =>
                {
                    (Target::Service(name.clone()), whole)
                }
                ReferenceForm::Service { .. } | ReferenceForm::Unreadable => return None,
            }
        }
    };
    Some(ReferenceTarget {
        reference,
        range,
        target,
    })
}

/// Every reference of the model with its slot. References never overlap, so
/// the order does not decide which one a cursor is on.
fn written_references(system: &CheckedSystem) -> Vec<(&Reference, Slot<'_>)> {
    let mut references = Vec::new();
    let members = system
        .systems
        .iter()
        .map(|decl| (&decl.package, &decl.members, false))
        .chain(
            system
                .distributions
                .iter()
                .map(|decl| (&decl.package, &decl.members, false)),
        )
        .chain(system.deployments.iter().flat_map(|decl| {
            decl.machines
                .iter()
                .map(move |machine| (&decl.package, &machine.members, true))
        }));
    for (package, lines, placement) in members {
        for line in lines {
            let slot = Slot::Member { package, placement };
            references.push((&line.reference, slot));
        }
    }
    for (component, decl) in system.components.iter().enumerate() {
        for (line, member) in decl.offers.iter().enumerate() {
            references.push((&member.reference, Slot::Offers { component, line }));
        }
        for (line, member) in decl.requires.iter().enumerate() {
            references.push((&member.reference, Slot::Requires { component, line }));
        }
    }
    for decl in &system.deployments {
        if let Some(reference) = &decl.system {
            let package = &decl.package;
            references.push((reference, Slot::For { package }));
        }
    }
    references
}

/// The two parts of an instance reference `pkg.Name.inst`: from its start to
/// the end of `Name`, and the `inst` segment.
fn instance_ranges(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    reference: &Reference,
) -> Option<(TextRange, TextRange)> {
    let root = source_file(db, reference.site.file);
    let node = root
        .syntax()
        .covering_element(reference.site.range)
        .into_node()?;
    let segments: Vec<TextRange> = node
        .descendants_with_tokens()
        .filter_map(|element| element.into_token())
        .filter(|token| !token.kind().is_trivia() && token.kind() != SyntaxKind::Dot)
        .map(|token| token.text_range())
        .collect();
    let [.., name, instance] = segments.as_slice() else {
        return None;
    };
    Some((
        TextRange::new(reference.site.range.start(), name.end()),
        *instance,
    ))
}
```

Create `crates/ridl-lsp/src/rsdl.rs`:

````rust
//! Hover and go-to-definition in an `.rsdl` file (docs/ROADMAP.md epic E6.15,
//! rsdl reference §4).
//!
//! The cursor's reference and its target come from
//! [`ridl_sem::rsdl::reference_at`], which reads both from the checked system
//! model; this module only turns a target into a declaration site and into
//! hover markdown. A component, an instance and the system are declared in
//! `.rsdl` files and render from the model. An interface and a service are
//! declared in ridl and render as a hover on the ridl declaration does.

use ridl_core::db::InputFile;
use ridl_core::package::{Package, Workspace, package_of, service_catalog};
use ridl_sem::rsdl::{
    CheckedSystem, DeclAttrs, InterfaceId, MemberRef, Target, UNIT_INSTANCE, reference_at,
};
use ridl_sem::{Symbol, SymbolKind, check_package, check_system, resolve_package};
use ridl_syntax::ast::AstNode;
use rowan::{TextRange, TextSize};

use crate::hover::{self, HoverInfo};
use crate::nav;

/// The hover for the rsdl reference at `offset` in `file`, a file of `pkg`.
pub fn hover(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    pkg: Package,
    file: InputFile,
    offset: TextSize,
) -> Option<HoverInfo> {
    let system = check_system(db, ws, std);
    let found = reference_at(db, ws, std, &system, file, offset)?;
    let markdown = match &found.target {
        Target::System(index) => system_markdown(&system, *index),
        Target::Component(index) => component_markdown(&system, *index),
        Target::Instance {
            component,
            instance,
        } => instance_markdown(&system, *component, *instance)?,
        Target::Service(name) | Target::Interface(InterfaceId::Inline { service: name }) => {
            service_markdown(db, ws, std, name)?
        }
        Target::Interface(InterfaceId::Declared { package, name }) => {
            let symbol = interface_symbol(db, ws, std, package, name)?;
            hover::symbol_markdown(db, ws, std, pkg, &symbol)
        }
    };
    Some(HoverInfo {
        markdown,
        range: found.range,
    })
}

/// The declaration site of what the rsdl reference at `offset` in `file` names.
pub fn definition(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    file: InputFile,
    offset: TextSize,
) -> Option<(InputFile, TextRange)> {
    let system = check_system(db, ws, std);
    let found = reference_at(db, ws, std, &system, file, offset)?;
    let site = match &found.target {
        Target::System(index) => system.systems[*index].name.site,
        Target::Component(index) => system.components[*index].name.site,
        Target::Instance {
            component,
            instance,
        } => system.components[*component].instances.as_ref()?[*instance].site,
        Target::Service(name) | Target::Interface(InterfaceId::Inline { service: name }) => {
            return service_site(db, ws, std, name);
        }
        Target::Interface(InterfaceId::Declared { package, name }) => {
            let symbol = interface_symbol(db, ws, std, package, name)?;
            return Some((symbol.file, symbol.range));
        }
    };
    Some((site.file, site.range))
}

/// The declared `interface` `name` of `package`, read from that package's
/// resolved names as navigation on a ridl reference reads it.
fn interface_symbol(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    package: &str,
    name: &str,
) -> Option<Symbol> {
    let target = if package == std.name(db) {
        std
    } else {
        package_of(db, ws, package.to_string())?
    };
    resolve_package(db, ws, target, std)
        .symbols
        .get(name)
        .filter(|symbol| symbol.package == package && symbol.kind == SymbolKind::Interface)
        .cloned()
}

/// The declaring package of the service `name`, from the service catalog.
fn service_package(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    name: &str,
) -> Option<Package> {
    let entry = service_catalog(db, ws, std).entries.get(name)?.clone();
    package_of(db, ws, entry.package)
}

/// The dotted name of the `service` declaration the catalog holds for `name`:
/// the first one in file order, as the catalog keeps the first (RIDL-140).
fn service_site(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    name: &str,
) -> Option<(InputFile, TextRange)> {
    let package = service_package(db, ws, std, name)?;
    package.files(db).iter().find_map(|file| {
        nav::source_file(db, *file).services().find_map(|service| {
            let dotted = service.name()?;
            (dotted.text() == name).then(|| (*file, dotted.syntax().text_range()))
        })
    })
}

/// A ridl service as a hover on its own declaration renders it.
fn service_markdown(
    db: &dyn salsa::Database,
    ws: Workspace,
    std: Package,
    name: &str,
) -> Option<String> {
    let package = service_package(db, ws, std, name)?;
    let ir = check_package(db, ws, package, std).ir;
    let service = ir.services.iter().find(|service| service.name == name)?;
    Some(hover::render_service(service))
}

fn system_markdown(system: &CheckedSystem, index: usize) -> String {
    let decl = &system.systems[index];
    let mut out = format!("```rsdl\nsystem {}.{}\n```", decl.package, decl.name.name);
    push_lines(&mut out, "Members", &decl.members);
    push_attrs(&mut out, &decl.attrs);
    out
}

fn component_markdown(system: &CheckedSystem, index: usize) -> String {
    let decl = &system.components[index];
    let mut out = format!(
        "```rsdl\ncomponent {}.{}\n```",
        decl.package, decl.name.name
    );
    match &decl.instances {
        None => out.push_str(&format!(
            "\n\n**Instances:** the unit instance `{UNIT_INSTANCE}`"
        )),
        Some(instances) if !instances.is_empty() => {
            let names: Vec<String> = instances
                .iter()
                .map(|instance| format!("`{}`", instance.name))
                .collect();
            out.push_str(&format!("\n\n**Instances:** {}", names.join(", ")));
        }
        Some(_) => {}
    }
    push_lines(&mut out, "Offers", &decl.offers);
    push_lines(&mut out, "Requires", &decl.requires);
    if decl.external {
        out.push_str("\n\n**External:** no implementation in this workspace (rsdl §3.2)");
    }
    push_attrs(&mut out, &decl.attrs);
    out
}

fn instance_markdown(system: &CheckedSystem, component: usize, instance: usize) -> Option<String> {
    let decl = &system.components[component];
    let name = &decl.instances.as_ref()?[instance].name;
    Some(format!(
        "```rsdl\n{}.{}.{name}\n```\n\nInstance `{name}` of the component `{}` (rsdl §7)",
        decl.package, decl.name.name, decl.name.name
    ))
}

/// `**Label:** `a`, `b`` over the references of `lines`, as written.
fn push_lines(out: &mut String, label: &str, lines: &[MemberRef]) {
    if lines.is_empty() {
        return;
    }
    let names: Vec<String> = lines
        .iter()
        .map(|line| format!("`{}`", line.reference.text()))
        .collect();
    out.push_str(&format!("\n\n**{label}:** {}", names.join(", ")));
}

/// The family attributes, in the order a ridl declaration's hover shows them.
fn push_attrs(out: &mut String, attrs: &DeclAttrs) {
    if !attrs.labels.is_empty() {
        out.push_str(&format!("\n\n**Labels:** {}", attrs.labels.join(", ")));
    }
    if let Some(reason) = &attrs.deprecated {
        out.push_str(&format!("\n\n**Deprecated:** {reason}"));
    }
}
````

### Task 10: The VS Code extension, the MCP server and `ridl fmt` on `.rsdl`

**Model:** Opus. **Story:** E6.15. **Spec:** rsdl reference §2 (files and
keywords), §3 and Appendix A (the examples), §5 (the attribute block); ADR-0010
decision 1 (the `ridl fmt` exit codes).

This task gives `.rsdl` files the three remaining editor-side entries: the VS
Code extension's language, grammar and language client, the MCP server's `rsdl`
profile, and `ridl fmt`, which replaces Task 1's refusal.

**Decisions in this task.**

- **`ridl fmt` formats `.rsdl` files.** From the code of `crates/ridl-fmt`:
  `format` parses under the profile it is given and returns a file with any
  parse error untouched; `format_element` has layout rules for the typl nodes
  only, and any other node — a ridl `interface` or `service` today, and each of
  the five rsdl declarations — takes the fallback arm, which emits the node's
  own text; `block_kind` counts that node as a member, so the file layout puts
  one blank line between it and its neighbours and keeps comments on their
  lines. Every rsdl declaration is a direct child of the file (a `machine` is
  inside its deployment's text), so the fallback always runs at indent 0 and
  emits the text unchanged. The round-trip test over the reference's §3 and
  Appendix A examples passes with no change to the formatter: a second pass
  changes nothing and the non-trivia tokens are the source's. `.rsdl` therefore
  gets the guarantee `.ridl` already has. The explicit-path refusal goes, the
  directory walk collects `.rsdl` files, and ADR-0010's `ridl fmt` row is not
  edited: an `.rsdl` file exits 0 or 1 as a `.typl` file does, and exit 2 keeps
  its two causes.
- **What `ridl fmt` does not do inside an rsdl declaration, debt to file.** It
  applies no layout there: Appendix A's column alignment
  (`offers   veh.adas.cruise`) and separator commas stay as written, although
  general form §5 forbids alignment. The same holds for ridl `interface` and
  `service` declarations today. The executor files one issue, "`ridl fmt` has no
  layout rules for the ridl and rsdl declarations", and the CLI reference says
  so.
- **The tests read their examples from the rsdl reference**, as Task 8 reads
  §16.1. They rely on Appendix A declaring `package veh.topology`, as the pull
  request that added this plan made it: `package veh.system` is FORM-101 in
  every profile, because `system` is reserved.
- **The MCP server's `rsdl` profile checks one text as `ridlc::check_source`
  does**: parse, resolve and `check_package`, with no `check_system`. This task
  does not add the system query there: on one text the catalog is empty, so
  every `offers` line would draw RSDL-310. The tool description, the `Profile`
  doc comment and the crate README say which checks run.
- **The grammar includes the typl patterns by reference**, as the ridl grammar
  does, and adds the eight rsdl keywords and the attribute block of rsdl §5
  (rsdl-owned keys, `namespace.key` backend keys). No typl declaration keyword
  and no retired v0.1 word is highlighted as a keyword.
- **The client's start rule and document selector read one list.**
  `clientDocumentSelector()` in `binaryResolution.ts` maps
  `CLIENT_LANGUAGE_IDS`, so the pure-logic tests pin both.
- **The extension's display name and description name rsdl**
  (`RIDL (typl + ridl + rsdl)`); no marketplace release exists yet.

**Shared file.** `crates/ridl/src/main.rs` (S1 and S2 → L4 → #324). Step 1
checks it.

Create `crates/ridl-fmt/tests/rsdl.rs`:

````rust
//! The formatter over rsdl files (rsdl reference v0.2). It has no layout rules
//! of its own for the five rsdl declarations: it lays out the file — the
//! header, one blank line between declarations, the comments — and emits each
//! declaration as written, as it does a ridl `interface` or `service`. These
//! tests hold that to two properties over the reference's own examples, and pin
//! what the layout changes.

use ridl_fmt::{FormatOutcome, format};
use ridl_syntax::{Profile, SyntaxKind, parse};

/// The rsdl reference, read at test time so that the examples are its own.
fn reference() -> String {
    let path = concat!(
        env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
        "/../../docs/specification/rsdl-language-reference.md"
    );
    std::fs::read_to_string(path).expect("the rsdl reference is readable")
}

/// The `rsdl` fenced blocks between the headings `from` and `to`, each as a
/// whole file: a block with no `package` line is given one.
fn examples(reference: &str, from: &str, to: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let section = reference
        .split_once(from)
        .and_then(|(_, rest)| rest.split_once(to))
        .map(|(section, _)| section)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("the reference has `{from}` before `{to}`"));
    let mut blocks = Vec::new();
    let mut current: Option<String> = None;
    for line in section.lines() {
        match current.as_mut() {
            None if line == "```rsdl" => current = Some(String::new()),
            None => {}
            Some(_) if line == "```" => {
                let block = current.take().unwrap_or_default();
                if block.starts_with("package ") {
                    blocks.push(block);
                } else {
                    blocks.push(format!("package veh.topology\n\n{block}"));
                }
            }
            Some(block) => {
                block.push_str(line);
                block.push('\n');
            }
        }
    }
    blocks
}

/// The kind and text of every non-trivia token of `text`, parsed as rsdl.
fn tokens(text: &str) -> Vec<(SyntaxKind, String)> {
    parse(text, Profile::Rsdl)
        .syntax()
        .descendants_with_tokens()
        .filter_map(|element| element.into_token())
        .filter(|token| !token.kind().is_trivia())
        .map(|token| (token.kind(), token.text().to_string()))
        .collect()
}

fn formatted(text: &str) -> String {
    match format(text, Profile::Rsdl) {
        FormatOutcome::Formatted(out) => out,
        FormatOutcome::ParseErrors(errors) => panic!("the example parses: {errors:?}\n{text}"),
    }
}

#[test]
fn the_reference_rsdl_examples_format_idempotently_and_keep_every_token() {
    let reference = reference();
    let mut sources = examples(
        &reference,
        "## 3. The Five Declarations",
        "## 4. Member Lines",
    );
    let declarations = sources.len();
    sources.extend(examples(&reference, "## Appendix A", "## Appendix B"));
    assert_eq!(
        (declarations, sources.len()),
        (4, 7),
        "§3 holds four rsdl examples and Appendix A three"
    );
    for source in &sources {
        let once = formatted(source);
        assert_eq!(
            formatted(&once),
            once,
            "a second pass changes nothing:\n{source}"
        );
        assert_eq!(tokens(&once), tokens(source), "no token changes:\n{source}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn an_rsdl_file_takes_the_file_layout_and_keeps_its_declarations_as_written() {
    let source = "package veh.topology\n\nimport veh.adas.LaneAssist\n\n\n\
        /// Two copies.\n\
        component Cruise [ instances = (primary, backup) ] { requires LaneAssist }\n\
        system   Vehicle { Cruise }\n";
    let expected = "package veh.topology\nimport veh.adas.LaneAssist\n\n\
        /// Two copies.\n\
        component Cruise [ instances = (primary, backup) ] { requires LaneAssist }\n\n\
        system   Vehicle { Cruise }\n";
    assert_eq!(formatted(source), expected);
}
````

In `crates/ridl/tests/facade.rs`, replace `fmt_refuses_an_rsdl_file` with its
doc comment (lines 128-144) with:

```rust
/// `ridl fmt` formats an explicit `.rsdl` path under the rsdl profile: the file
/// layout is applied (one blank line between declarations) and each rsdl
/// declaration is kept as written, so a second `--check` pass changes nothing.
#[test]
fn fmt_formats_an_rsdl_file() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("fmt-rsdl");
    let input = "package veh.topology\n\ncomponent   Cruise {}\nsystem Vehicle { Cruise }\n";
    let file = dir.write("system.rsdl", input);

    let (code, stderr) = ridl(&["fmt".as_ref(), file.as_os_str()]);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "the .rsdl file formats, stderr:\n{stderr}");
    let formatted = std::fs::read_to_string(&file).expect("the file is still readable");
    assert_eq!(
        formatted,
        "package veh.topology\n\ncomponent   Cruise {}\n\nsystem Vehicle { Cruise }\n"
    );

    let (code, stderr) = ridl(&["fmt".as_ref(), "--check".as_ref(), file.as_os_str()]);
    assert_eq!(
        code, 0,
        "the formatted file is a fixed point, stderr:\n{stderr}"
    );
}

/// A directory walk collects `.rsdl` files: `ridl fmt --check` over a tree
/// whose one file to change is an `.rsdl` file exits 1.
#[test]
fn fmt_check_walks_into_rsdl_files() {
    let dir = TempDir::new("fmt-rsdl-walk");
    let input = "package veh.topology\n\ncomponent Cruise {}\nsystem Vehicle { Cruise }\n";
    let file = dir.write("system.rsdl", input);

    let (code, stderr) = ridl(&["fmt".as_ref(), "--check".as_ref(), dir.path().as_os_str()]);
    assert_eq!(code, 1, "the .rsdl file would change, stderr:\n{stderr}");
    let unchanged = std::fs::read_to_string(&file).expect("the file is still readable");
    assert_eq!(unchanged, input, "`--check` writes nothing");
}
```

    #[test]
    fn check_parses_an_rsdl_source_under_the_rsdl_profile() {
        let component = "package p\n\ncomponent Idle {}\n".to_string();
        let rsdl = check(&CheckParams {
            source: component.clone(),
            profile: Profile::Rsdl,
        });
        let typl = check(&CheckParams {
            source: component,
            profile: Profile::Typl,
        });
        assert!(rsdl.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| d.severity != "error"));
        assert!(typl.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.severity == "error"));

        // rsdl reference §2: `internal` before an rsdl keyword is FORM-102.
        let internal = check(&CheckParams {
            source: "package p\n\ninternal component Idle {}\n".to_string(),
            profile: Profile::Rsdl,
        });
        let error = internal
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.severity == "error")
            .expect("an error diagnostic");
        assert_eq!(error.code, "FORM-102");
        assert_eq!(error.span.path, "input.rsdl");
    }

In `crates/ridl-mcp/src/lib.rs`, replace the comment, doc comment and enum

```rust
// This enum is the only gate on the profile, and its doc comment is the
// description the tool's JSON schema carries to an agent. `ridlc` reads the
// profile off the file extension and treats every extension that is not
// `.ridl` or `.rsdl` as typl, so a profile name this enum did not reject
// would be checked as typl rather than refused.
/// Which language a source text is parsed as. These are the three profiles the
/// compiler has; the `.rxdl` form does not exist yet (epic E3.5). An rsdl text
/// is parsed and its names are resolved, but the rsdl system checks read the
/// whole workspace and do not run on one text.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
#[schemars(crate = "rmcp::schemars")]
pub enum Profile {
    Typl,
    Ridl,
    Rsdl,
}
```

```ts
import { strict as assert } from "node:assert";
import * as fs from "node:fs";
import * as path from "node:path";
import { test } from "node:test";

// The compiled test runs from out/, so the extension root is one level up.
const ROOT = path.join(__dirname, "..");

function readJson(relative: string): any {
  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(ROOT, relative), "utf8"));
}

// rsdl reference §2: the eight rsdl keywords, and the rsdl v0.1 words it retires.
const RSDL_KEYWORDS = ["system", "component", "distribution", "deployment", "machine", "offers", "requires", "for"];
const RETIRED_WORDS = [
  "provides",
  "instance",
  "assurance",
  "target",
  "place",
  "on",
  "transport",
  "bundle",
  "time",
  "base",
  "redundant",
  "supervise",
  "degraded",
];

test("the manifest contributes the rsdl language with its grammar", () => {
  const manifest = readJson("package.json");
  const language = manifest.contributes.languages.find((entry: { id: string }) => entry.id === "rsdl");
  assert.ok(language, "an rsdl language entry");
  assert.deepEqual(language.extensions, [".rsdl"]);
  const grammar = manifest.contributes.grammars.find((entry: { language: string }) => entry.language === "rsdl");
  assert.ok(grammar, "an rsdl grammar entry");
  assert.equal(grammar.scopeName, "source.rsdl");
  assert.equal(readJson(grammar.path).scopeName, "source.rsdl");
});

test("the rsdl grammar scopes the keys of an attribute block", () => {
  const grammar = readJson("syntaxes/rsdl.tmLanguage.json");
  const includes = (patterns: { include?: string }[]) => patterns.map((pattern) => pattern.include);
  assert.ok(includes(grammar.patterns).includes("#attributes"), "the file includes the attribute block");
  const attributes = grammar.repository.attributes;
  assert.deepEqual([attributes.begin, attributes.end], ["\\[", "\\]"]);
  assert.ok(includes(attributes.patterns).includes("#backend-key"), "a block holds backend keys");
  assert.ok(includes(attributes.patterns).includes("#owned-key"), "a block holds rsdl-owned keys");
  assert.ok(new RegExp(grammar.repository["backend-key"].match).test("linux.cpuset"), "`linux.cpuset`");
  const owned = new RegExp(`^(?:${grammar.repository["owned-key"].match})$`);
  for (const key of ["instances", "external", "tier", "labels", "deprecated"]) {
    assert.ok(owned.test(key), `\`${key}\` is an rsdl-owned key`);
  }
});

test("the rsdl grammar highlights the rsdl keywords and no retired word", () => {
  const grammar = readJson("syntaxes/rsdl.tmLanguage.json");
  const keywordRules = Object.entries(grammar.repository)
    .filter(([name]) => name.startsWith("keywords-"))
    .map(([, rule]) => new RegExp(`^(?:${(rule as { match: string }).match})$`));
  const highlighted = (word: string) => keywordRules.some((rule) => rule.test(word));
  for (const word of RSDL_KEYWORDS) {
    assert.ok(highlighted(word), `\`${word}\` is highlighted as a keyword`);
  }
  for (const word of RETIRED_WORDS) {
    assert.ok(!highlighted(word), `\`${word}\` is not highlighted as a keyword`);
  }
});
```

{
  "name": "rsdl",
  "scopeName": "source.rsdl",

    "An .rsdl file holds the five rsdl declarations over the typl file structure, so",
    "this grammar includes the typl patterns it shares by reference (source.typl#...)",
    "rather than copying them. What it adds is the eight rsdl keywords of rsdl reference",
    "§2 (system component distribution deployment machine offers requires for) and the",
    "attribute block of rsdl §5, with its rsdl-owned keys and its `namespace.key`",
    "backend keys. Update this grammar and keywords.rs together when the registry changes."
  ],
  "patterns": [
    { "include": "source.typl#comments" },
    { "include": "source.typl#strings" },
    { "include": "#attributes" },
    { "include": "source.typl#numbers" },
    { "include": "#keywords-declaration" },
    { "include": "#keywords-line" },
    { "include": "#keywords-clause" },
    { "include": "source.typl#keywords-control" },
    { "include": "source.typl#keywords-modifier" },
    { "include": "source.typl#constants-language" },
    { "include": "source.typl#screaming-snake" },
    { "include": "source.typl#type-names" },
    { "include": "source.typl#punctuation" }
  ],
  "repository": {
    "keywords-declaration": {
      "comment": "rsdl §3 — the five declarations.",
      "match": "\\b(system|component|distribution|deployment|machine)\\b",
      "name": "storage.type.rsdl"
    },
    "keywords-line": {
      "comment": "rsdl §4 — the keyword of a component line.",
      "match": "\\b(offers|requires)\\b",
      "name": "keyword.other.line.rsdl"
    },
    "keywords-clause": {
      "comment": "rsdl §3.4 — the relation clause of a deployment.",
      "match": "\\bfor\\b",
      "name": "keyword.other.clause.rsdl"
    },
    "attributes": {
      "comment": "rsdl §5 — the attribute block after a name or at the end of a line.",
      "begin": "\\[",
      "beginCaptures": {
        "0": { "name": "punctuation.section.brackets.begin.rsdl" }
      },
      "end": "\\]",
      "endCaptures": {
        "0": { "name": "punctuation.section.brackets.end.rsdl" }
      },
      "patterns": [
        { "include": "source.typl#comments" },
        { "include": "source.typl#strings" },
        { "include": "source.typl#numbers" },
        { "include": "source.typl#constants-language" },
        { "include": "#backend-key" },

        { "include": "source.typl#screaming-snake" },
        { "include": "source.typl#punctuation" }

      "match": "\\b([a-z][A-Za-z0-9]*)(\\.)([a-z][A-Za-z0-9]*)\\b",
      "captures": {
        "1": { "name": "entity.name.namespace.rsdl" },

        "3": { "name": "variable.other.property.rsdl" }
      }
    },
    "owned-key": {
      "comment": "rsdl §5 — the rsdl-owned keys and the family keys `labels` and `deprecated`.",
      "match": "\\b(instances|external|tier|labels|deprecated)\\b",
      "name": "variable.other.property.rsdl"
    }
  }
}

## 5. Part B4 — lowering, `ridl diff` and the book chapter

| Task | Story | Work                                                                                         | Model |
| ---- | ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| 1    | E6.16 | `system.proto`: the `System` message and its encodings                                       | Fable |
| 2    | E6.16 | closure facts: components, instances, producers, attribute maps, labels                      | Fable |
| 3    | E6.16 | per-deployment facts: machines, placement, the link set with crossing kinds, the surface set | Fable |
| 4    | E6.16 | the routing table, the region map without its hash, the permission list                      | Fable |
| 5    | E6.16 | distribution installation and dependency                                                     | Fable |
| 6    | E6.16 | the pipeline: `ridlc` emits the system IR; error gating per rsdl §13                         | Opus  |
| 7    | E6.18 | `ridl diff` at the system: "placement changed" and "composition changed"                     | Fable |
| 8    | E6.19 | the book chapter; the harness learns `rsdl` fences; CONTRIBUTING and AGENTS.md               | Opus  |
| 9    | E6.17 | the catalog hash in each region; `sdd-gardening` over lane B's records                       | Fable |

### The IR design

`crates/ridl-ir/proto/ridl/ir/v2/system.proto`, package `ridl.ir.v2`, one
artifact per workspace named `<pkg.Name>.system.{json,txtpb,binpb}`:

    System              name, package, labels, attributes; members, components, producers, grants, regions, distributions, deployments
      MemberLine        a member line of a system or a distribution: component (qualified), attributes
      Component         name, package ("" when implicit), implicit, external, instances ("Unit"), offers, requires, labels, attributes
        Offer           service, attributes (the line's)
        Require         interface, service (owning), producer (qualified), attributes (the line's, the link's site)
          InterfaceRef  catalog (declaring package), name (interface name or service dotted name), inline
      Producer          service, component, instances, not_yet_realizable (more than one instance, RSDL-409)
      Grant             component, external, regions (catalog names)
      Region            catalog, interfaces (Task 9 adds hash)
        RegionInterface name, inline, number, provisional, service (owning)
      Distribution      name, package, members, depends_on (qualified), labels, attributes
      Deployment        name, package, labels, attributes; machines, placements, links, routes, surface, installations
        Machine         name, external, labels, attributes
        Placement       component, instance, machine, attributes (the placement line's)
        Link            interface, service, consumer, producer, crossing
          Endpoint      component, instance, machine
          Crossing      SAME_MACHINE | DIFFERENT_MACHINE | OFF_BOARD
        Route           catalog, interface_number, member_ordinal (the key); interface, member, service; producers (Endpoint)
        Surface         link, direction (EXTERNAL_CONSUMES | EXTERNAL_OFFERS)
        Installation    distribution (qualified), machines
      Attribute         namespace, key, value (absent for a flag)
        AttributeValue  oneof scalar | list
        AttributeList   items

The closure facts are stated once; the per-deployment facts once per deployment
that no RSDL-7xx error blocked. A producer's machines are read from the
deployment's placements. `tier` and `deprecated` are not lowered: rsdl §5
consumes them into checks, and §13 lists neither.

### Task 1: The `System` message and its three encodings

**Model:** Fable. **Story:** E6.16. **Spec:** rsdl reference §13 (what the
lowering produces), §5 (attributes; `labels` beside the map), §7 (`Unit`), §10
(crossing kinds), §11 (grants, regions), §1.3 (no rsdl name is a wire identity);
ADR-0014 decisions 1, 4, 7, 9, 12 and 14 (the three encodings); plan decision
P-B1.

This task adds the rsdl system layer to the IR: a new schema file `system.proto`
in `ridl.ir.v2`, compiled by the same `protox` pass as `ir.proto`, and the three
encodings of its root message `System` beside the package's. Nothing produces a
`System` yet (Tasks 2 to 5) and nothing writes one (Task 6).

**Inputs from other lanes.** The routing key and the region map read
`Interface.number` and `Interface.provisional`, which lane L's implementation
adds to `ir.proto` (lock design §9: `uint32 number = 7`, `bool provisional = 8`,
set for inline shapes too; `Package.retired = 5`). B4 starts after G4, so the
fields exist when this task runs. The catalog hash is not in this task: it comes
from #324 (P-B2) and Task 9 adds its field.

**Decisions in this task.**

- **One `System` per workspace, its own artifact.** A workspace declares at most
  one `system` (rsdl §1.5), so the artifact is named after it:
  `<pkg.Name>.system.json`, `.system.txtpb`, `.system.binpb`. A package name is
  lowercase and ends in `.ir.*`; a system's qualified name ends in a CamelCase
  segment and `.system.*`, so the two never collide in one output directory.
  Task 6 writes it.
- **The closure facts are stated once, the per-deployment facts once per
  deployment.** rsdl §13 lists the producers under "per deployment" with the
  machine of each instance; the machine is a placement fact, so a `Producer`
  names the component and its instances and the machines are read from
  `Deployment.placements`, which lists every instance. Nothing is lost and no
  fact is repeated.
- **§13's facts and nothing else.** `tier` and `deprecated` are rsdl-owned keys
  consumed into checks (RSDL-901, the family lint, rsdl §5), and §13 lowers
  neither. `labels` ride beside the attribute map on every declaration (§13).
  The model carries no doc comment for an rsdl declaration, so the IR has no
  `doc` field.
- **References are by qualified name.** A component is `pkg.Name`, or the
  service's dotted name for the implicit component (rsdl §6); the case of the
  last segment tells the two apart (CamelCase against lowercase). A machine name
  is bare, scoped to its deployment (§3.5). `System`, `Component` and
  `Distribution` gain `qualified_name()` in `lib.rs`, the one derivation.
- **An interface carries an `inline` flag beside its name.** The inline shape of
  `service cabin` and `interface cabin` may be spelled the same in one package;
  the lock keeps them apart with its `service:` prefix (lock design §3), and
  `InterfaceRef` and `RegionInterface` keep them apart with `inline`.
- **`MemberLine`, not `Member`.** In ridl a member is one typed interaction of
  an interface (rsdl §1.3); the message for a `system` or `distribution` body
  line takes the longer name.
- **An attribute value is written text or a list.** `AttributeValue` mirrors the
  model's `WrittenValue` (a scalar's text as written, quotes included, or a
  parenthesised list), so a backend key is carried uninterpreted (rsdl §5).

**Files:**

- Create: `crates/ridl-ir/proto/ridl/ir/v2/system.proto`
- Modify: `crates/ridl-ir/build.rs` (the module comment, lines 3-5; `protos`,
  line 20)
- Modify: `crates/ridl-ir/src/lib.rs` (`package_descriptor` and `transcode`,
  near line 53; `to_json_pretty`, `from_json` and `parse_json`, near lines
  157-285; `to_text_format`, near line 300; `from_text_format`, near line 371;
  after `from_binary`, near line 389)
- Test: `crates/ridl-ir/src/lib.rs` (a new `system_round_trip` module at the end
  of the file)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ridl_ir::v2::{SerializeError, TextFormatError}` and the private

- Produces, in `ridl_ir::v2`:

    `Distribution::qualified_name(&self) -> String` — `pkg.Name`, or the bare

            "an unknown field is rejected"
        );
    }

    #[test]

        let system = fixture();
        let text = v2::system_to_text_format(&system).expect("the fixture serializes as prototext");
        assert!(
            text.starts_with("name:"),
            "fields print in schema index order, got: {text}"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            system,
            v2::system_from_text_format(&text).expect("prototext parsing must succeed")
        );
    }

        let system = fixture();
        assert_eq!(system.qualified_name(), "veh.topology.Vehicle");
        assert_eq!(system.components[0].qualified_name(), "veh.topology.Cruise");
        assert_eq!(system.components[1].qualified_name(), "veh.diag.access");
        assert_eq!(
            system.distributions[0].qualified_name(),
            "veh.topology.Adas"

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the schema**

Create `crates/ridl-ir/proto/ridl/ir/v2/system.proto`:

```proto
syntax = "proto3";

// IR v2 — the rsdl system layer (rsdl language reference §13).
//
// One `System` message per workspace: the facts the rsdl lowering derives
// from the checked system, once for the closure (rsdl §3.1) and once per
// `deployment` (§3.4). It is its own artifact beside the package IR of
// `ir.proto`: a package carries contracts, a system carries who produces
// them, where each copy runs, and every link between a consumer and a
// producer with its crossing kind. A runtime's descriptor is an emitter over
// these facts and is specified with the runtime (rsdl §13).
//
// The artifact is named after the system's qualified name, `pkg.Name`, with
// the suffix of its encoding: `<pkg.Name>.system.json`, `.system.txtpb` or
// `.system.binpb` (ADR-0014 decision 4), written by the same `--emit` value
// that writes the package IR in that encoding.
//
// Every fact of rsdl §13 has a field here, and nothing else does. The
// closure-level facts are stated once rather than per deployment:
//
//   closure     components with their lines, the system's member lines,
//               producers, the permission list, the region map,
//               distributions with their dependency
//   deployment  machines, the placement of every instance, the link set,
//               the routing table, the surface set, distribution
//               installation
//
// A producer's machines are read from the deployment's placements, not
// repeated on the producer. The rsdl-owned keys are consumed into the facts
// they feed (rsdl §5): `instances` and `external` are fields, `tier` and
// `deprecated` feed checks (RSDL-901, the family lint) and are not lowered.
//
// Names. A component is referred to by its qualified name — `pkg.Name` for a
// declared component, the service's dotted name for the implicit component
// of a lone service (rsdl §6) — and `Component::qualified_name` in `lib.rs`
// is the one derivation. A distribution and a system are qualified the same
// way. A machine name is scoped to its deployment (rsdl §3.5) and is written
// bare. An interface is referred to by its catalog (the package that
// declares it, vocabulary V-16), its name — the interface's own name, or the
// owning service's dotted name for an inline shape (ridl §14.5) — and
// whether it is inline, because the two names may be spelled the same. None
// of these names is a wire identity (rsdl §1.3): the routing key is
// (catalog, interface number, member ordinal), and the interface numbers
// come from each package's lock through `Interface.number` in `ir.proto`.
//
// Field numbering follows `ir.proto`: data fields start at 1; oneof members
// start at 10.
package ridl.ir.v2;

// The lowered system: the closure facts, then one `Deployment` per
// deployment the lowering was not blocked for (rsdl §13, "Errors and
// warnings").
message System {
  // CamelCase name of the `system` declaration (rsdl §3.1).
  string name = 1;
  // The package that declares it.
  string package = 2;
  // `labels` of the declaration, carried beside the attribute map (rsdl §5).
  repeated string labels = 3;
  // The declaration's backend keys, as declared (rsdl §5, §13).
  repeated Attribute attributes = 4;
  // The system's member lines in source order, each naming one closure
  // component (rsdl §4).
  repeated MemberLine members = 5;
  // The closure (rsdl §3.1): the listed components and the implicit
  // components of the listed services, in the order the system lists them.
  repeated Component components = 6;
  // For every service in the closure, its offering component and instances
  // (rsdl §8, §13), in service name order.
  repeated Producer producers = 7;
  // The permission list (rsdl §11, §13): one grant per closure component,
  // in closure order.
  repeated Grant grants = 8;
  // The region map (rsdl §13): one region per catalog the closure reaches,
  // in catalog name order.
  repeated Region regions = 9;
  // Every distribution of the workspace, in package, then file, then source
  // order, with its dependency (rsdl §3.3, §13). Empty when the workspace
  // declares none.
  repeated Distribution distributions = 10;
  // Every deployment `for` the system whose lowering no RSDL-7xx error
  // blocked, in package, then file, then source order (rsdl §13).
  repeated Deployment deployments = 11;
}

// A member line of a `system` or a `distribution` body (rsdl §4): the
// component it names and the line's backend keys.
message MemberLine {
  // The qualified component name.
  string component = 1;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 2;
}

// One component of the closure (rsdl §3.2, §6, §7).
message Component {
  // The declared CamelCase name, or the service's dotted name for an
  // implicit component.
  string name = 1;
  // The declaring package; empty for an implicit component, which no
  // package declares (rsdl §6).
  string package = 2;
  // True for the implicit component of a lone service (rsdl §6).
  bool implicit = 3;
  // The `external` flag (rsdl §3.2, §9): no implementation in this
  // workspace.
  bool external = 4;
  // The instance names in declaration order, or the one unit instance
  // `Unit` (rsdl §7).
  repeated string instances = 5;
  // The `offers` lines in source order; an implicit component offers its
  // one service (rsdl §3.2, §6).
  repeated Offer offers = 6;
  // The `requires` lines in source order, each resolved (rsdl §8).
  repeated Require requires = 7;
  // `labels` of the declaration (rsdl §5).
  repeated string labels = 8;
  // The declaration's backend keys (rsdl §5).
  repeated Attribute attributes = 9;
}

// An `offers` line (rsdl §3.2).
message Offer {
  // The service's dotted name.
  string service = 1;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 2;
}

// A `requires` line, resolved over the closure (rsdl §8): the interface it
// names, the one closure service that lists it, and the one closure
// component that offers that service. The line is the attribute site of
// every link derived from it (rsdl §13).
message Require {
  InterfaceRef interface = 1;
  // The owning service's dotted name.
  string service = 2;
  // The offering component's qualified name.
  string producer = 3;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 4;
}

// An interface by identity (rsdl §11, vocabulary V-16): the catalog that
// numbers it and its name in that catalog.
message InterfaceRef {
  // The package that declares the interface — for an inline shape, the
  // package that declares the service.
  string catalog = 1;
  // The interface's name, or the owning service's dotted name for an
  // inline shape (ridl §14.5).
  string name = 2;
  // True for the inline shape of a service.
  bool inline = 3;
}

// The producer of one closure service (rsdl §8, §13).
message Producer {
  string service = 1;
  // The offering component's qualified name.
  string component = 2;
  // Its instances (rsdl §7); the machine each is placed on in a deployment
  // is in that deployment's `placements`.
  repeated string instances = 3;
  // True when the component has more than one instance: a redundant
  // provider set, which RSDL-409 reports not yet realizable (rsdl §7).
  bool not_yet_realizable = 4;
}

// One component's grant (rsdl §11): the catalog regions its `requires`
// lines reach. The write side is not a second list; it is read from
// `System.producers` (rsdl §13).
message Grant {
  // The component's qualified name.
  string component = 1;
  // The component's `external` flag: the grant is derived and lowered, and
  // enforcing it is outside this runtime (rsdl §11).
  bool external = 2;
  // The catalog names, in name order; empty for a component that requires
  // nothing.
  repeated string regions = 3;
}

// One region of the region map (rsdl §13): a catalog the closure reaches.
message Region {
  // The catalog's name — its package name (vocabulary V-16).
  string catalog = 1;
  // The interfaces of this catalog that closure services list, in interface
  // number order.
  repeated RegionInterface interfaces = 2;
}

// One interface of a region, with the identity the routing key uses.
message RegionInterface {
  // The interface's name in the catalog, or the owning service's dotted name
  // for an inline shape.
  string name = 1;
  // True for the inline shape of a service.
  bool inline = 2;
  // The interface number from the package's lock (`Interface.number`).
  uint32 number = 3;
  // True when the number has no lock entry yet (`Interface.provisional`).
  bool provisional = 4;
  // The closure service that lists the interface (rsdl §8).
  string service = 5;
}

// A `distribution` declaration with its derived dependency (rsdl §3.3, §13).
message Distribution {
  string name = 1;
  string package = 2;
  // The member lines in source order.
  repeated MemberLine members = 3;
  // The qualified names of the distributions this one depends on, in name
  // order: `A` depends on `B` when a component in `A` requires an interface
  // whose owning service a component in `B` offers (rsdl §13). The same in
  // every deployment.
  repeated string depends_on = 4;
  repeated string labels = 5;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 6;
}

// One placement of the system (rsdl §3.4, §9, §10, §13).
message Deployment {
  string name = 1;
  string package = 2;
  repeated string labels = 3;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 4;
  // Every machine of the deployment, in declaration order.
  repeated Machine machines = 5;
  // Every instance of every closure component, in closure order then
  // instance order, with the machine it is placed on (rsdl §9) — an
  // instance with no link included.
  repeated Placement placements = 6;
  // The link set (rsdl §8, §10): one link per (consumer instance, producer
  // instance) of every `requires` line, in `requires` order; a link with
  // two external endpoints is absent.
  repeated Link links = 7;
  // The routing table (rsdl §13), in (catalog, interface number, member
  // ordinal) order.
  repeated Route routes = 8;
  // The surface set (rsdl §13): every link with an external component at
  // exactly one endpoint, in link order.
  repeated Surface surface = 9;
  // Distribution installation (rsdl §13): one entry per distribution, in
  // `System.distributions` order. Empty when the workspace declares no
  // distribution.
  repeated Installation installations = 10;
}

// A `machine` declaration (rsdl §3.5).
message Machine {
  // Unique within its deployment.
  string name = 1;
  // True for a machine outside the built system (rsdl §3.5).
  bool external = 2;
  repeated string labels = 3;
  repeated Attribute attributes = 4;
}

// One instance on one machine (rsdl §9).
message Placement {
  // The component's qualified name.
  string component = 1;
  string instance = 2;
  string machine = 3;
  // The backend keys of the placement line that placed the instance (rsdl
  // §13).
  repeated Attribute attributes = 4;
}

// One end of a link: an instance and the machine it is placed on.
message Endpoint {
  // The component's qualified name.
  string component = 1;
  string instance = 2;
  string machine = 3;
}

// The crossing kind of a link (rsdl §10), derived from the two machines.
enum Crossing {
  CROSSING_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  // Both instances are on one machine.
  CROSSING_SAME_MACHINE = 1;
  // The machines differ and neither is external.
  CROSSING_DIFFERENT_MACHINE = 2;
  // The machines differ and at least one is external.
  CROSSING_OFF_BOARD = 3;
}

// One link (rsdl §8, §10): a consumer instance reaching a producer instance
// through a `requires` line. The line's attribute map is on the consumer
// component's `Require` entry.
message Link {
  InterfaceRef interface = 1;
  // The owning service's dotted name.
  string service = 2;
  Endpoint consumer = 3;
  Endpoint producer = 4;
  Crossing crossing = 5;
}

// One entry of the routing table (rsdl §13): the key (catalog, interface
// number, member ordinal), and the producing instances with their machines.
message Route {
  string catalog = 1;
  uint32 interface_number = 2;
  // The member's ordinal in the interface body (ridl §11, `Decl.ordinal`).
  uint32 member_ordinal = 3;
  // The interface's name in the catalog, beside the key, for a reader.
  string interface = 4;
  // The member's name, beside the key, for a reader.
  string member = 5;
  // The owning service's dotted name.
  string service = 6;
  repeated Endpoint producers = 7;
}

// Which side of a surface link is external (rsdl §13).
enum SurfaceDirection {
  SURFACE_DIRECTION_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  // The external component consumes; the producer is in the built system.
  SURFACE_DIRECTION_EXTERNAL_CONSUMES = 1;
  // The external component offers; the consumer is in the built system.
  SURFACE_DIRECTION_EXTERNAL_OFFERS = 2;
}

// One entry of the surface set (rsdl §13): the link, with the boundary read
// from the consumer's side.
message Surface {
  Link link = 1;
  SurfaceDirection direction = 2;
}

// Where one distribution is installed in a deployment (rsdl §13): the
// machines hosting at least one instance of its components.
message Installation {
  // The distribution's qualified name.
  string distribution = 1;
  // In machine declaration order.
  repeated string machines = 2;
}

// A backend key as declared (rsdl §5): `namespace.key`, a flag or a value,
// never interpreted by the compiler.
message Attribute {
  // `linux` in `linux.cpuset`.
  string namespace = 1;
  // `cpuset` in `linux.cpuset`.
  string key = 2;
  // Absent for a flag.
  AttributeValue value = 3;
}

// An attribute value as written (rsdl Appendix B `attr_value`): the text of
// one literal or name, or a parenthesised list.
message AttributeValue {
  oneof kind {
    string scalar = 10;
    AttributeList list = 11;
  }
}

message AttributeList {
  repeated AttributeValue items = 1;
}
```

In `crates/ridl-ir/src/lib.rs`, module `v2`, replace `package_descriptor` and
`transcode`:

```rust
    /// The descriptor of one root message of the compiled schema, by its
    /// full name.
    fn descriptor(name: &str) -> prost_reflect::MessageDescriptor {
        DESCRIPTOR_POOL
            .get_message_by_name(name)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name} is declared by the compiled schema"))
    }

    /// The `Package` message descriptor — the entry point of the prototext
    /// encoder and decoder, the one reflection path left in this module.
    pub(crate) fn package_descriptor() -> prost_reflect::MessageDescriptor {
        descriptor("ridl.ir.v2.Package")
    }

    /// The `System` message descriptor (`system.proto`), the prototext entry
    /// point of the rsdl system layer.
    pub(crate) fn system_descriptor() -> prost_reflect::MessageDescriptor {
        descriptor("ridl.ir.v2.System")
    }

    /// Rebuilds a message as a `DynamicMessage` over its descriptor — the
    /// step `prost-reflect` needs before rendering a text encoding.
    /// Transcoding goes through the wire encoding, whose decoder enforces
    /// prost's fixed recursion limit, so a package whose composite nesting
    /// crosses that limit fails here — an input-dependent failure, not
    /// schema drift (ADR-0014 decision 12).
    fn transcode<M: prost::Message>(
        descriptor: prost_reflect::MessageDescriptor,
        message: &M,
    ) -> Result<prost_reflect::DynamicMessage, prost::DecodeError> {
        let mut dynamic = prost_reflect::DynamicMessage::new(descriptor);
        dynamic.transcode_from(message)?;
        Ok(dynamic)
    }
```

Replace the body of `to_json_pretty` (its doc comment stays) with a call, and

```rust
    pub fn to_json_pretty(package: &Package) -> Result<String, SerializeError> {
        render_json(package)
    }

    /// Renders a lowered system (`system.proto`, rsdl reference §13) as
    /// pretty-printed canonical protobuf JSON — the `<pkg.Name>.system.json`
    /// artifact, under the rules of [`to_json_pretty`].
    pub fn system_to_json_pretty(system: &System) -> Result<String, SerializeError> {
        render_json(system)
    }

    /// The one JSON writer behind [`to_json_pretty`] and
    /// [`system_to_json_pretty`]: the pbjson-generated `Serialize` impl of
    /// the message, pretty-printed.
    fn render_json<M: serde::Serialize>(message: &M) -> Result<String, SerializeError> {
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        let mut serializer = serde_json::Serializer::pretty(&mut buf);
        serde::Serialize::serialize(message, &mut serializer).map_err(SerializeError::Json)?;
        Ok(String::from_utf8(buf).expect("serde_json emits UTF-8"))
    }

fn parse_json<M: serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(text: &str) -> Result<M, serde_json::Error> {
    let mut deserializer = serde_json::Deserializer::from_str(text);
    deserializer.disable_recursion_limit();
    let message: M = serde::Deserialize::deserialize(&mut deserializer)?;
    deserializer.end()?;
    Ok(message)

Insert after `from_binary`, before the `InterfaceShape` doc comment
(`/// One interface shape of a package (ridl §14.0)`):

```rust
    /// Encodes a lowered system in the protobuf binary wire format — the
    /// `<pkg.Name>.system.binpb` artifact (ADR-0014 decision 9).
    pub fn system_to_binary(system: &System) -> Vec<u8> {
        prost::Message::encode_to_vec(system)
    }

    /// Decodes a lowered system from the protobuf binary wire format — the
    /// inverse of [`system_to_binary`].
    pub fn system_from_binary(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<System, prost::DecodeError> {
        prost::Message::decode(bytes)
    }

    /// `pkg.Name` — how a system, a component or a distribution is referred
    /// to across the system layer (`system.proto`); a name with no package,
    /// the implicit component of a lone service (rsdl §6), is its own
    /// qualified name.
    fn qualified(package: &str, name: &str) -> String {
        if package.is_empty() {
            name.to_string()
        } else {
            format!("{package}.{name}")
        }
    }

    impl System {
        /// The system's qualified name, `pkg.Name` — the base name of its
        /// artifacts.
        pub fn qualified_name(&self) -> String {
            qualified(&self.package, &self.name)
        }
    }

    impl Component {
        /// The name every reference to this component uses: `pkg.Name` for a
        /// declared component, the service's dotted name for an implicit one.
        pub fn qualified_name(&self) -> String {
            qualified(&self.package, &self.name)
        }
    }

    impl Distribution {
        /// The name `Distribution.depends_on` and `Installation.distribution`
        /// use.
        pub fn qualified_name(&self) -> String {
            qualified(&self.package, &self.name)
        }
    }
