- [ ] **Step 5: Compare the systems**

Insert above the test module of `crates/ridl-diff/src/system.rs`:

```rust
//! `ridl diff` at the system (rsdl reference §14): the changes to a lowered
//! system that are not contract changes.
//!
//! The contracts of a workspace are compared by the ridl categories, through
//! [`crate::diff_sets`]. What rsdl adds is structure — which components exist,
//! their lines, and where each instance runs — and a change to it changes the
//! derived links and never the contract. Such a change is listed under one of
//! two headings and carries no verdict: neither compatible nor breaking. Which
//! of them are breaking, and for whom, is the stability policy's (roadmap
//! E4.5a).
//!
//! - **Placement changed** — a change in a deployment: a deployment added or
//!   removed, a machine added, removed or made `external`, an instance moved to
//!   another machine.
//! - **Composition changed** — a change to the closure or to a component's
//!   lines: a component added to or removed from the system, an `offers` or
//!   `requires` line added or removed, `instances` changed, a component made
//!   `external`.
//!
//! Both read the lowered `System` of each side and nothing else.

use ridl_ir::v2::{Component, Deployment, InterfaceRef, System};

/// The heading a system change is listed under (rsdl reference §14).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum SystemHeading {
    /// A change in a deployment.
    PlacementChanged,
    /// A change to the closure or to a component's lines.
    CompositionChanged,
}

/// One change to the system, listed under its heading with no verdict.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SystemChange {
    pub heading: SystemHeading,
    /// A slash-separated path: a component's qualified name, then `offers`,
    /// `requires`, `instances` or `external`; or a deployment's name, then a
    /// machine or an instance.
    pub path: String,
    /// The rendered old value, absent when the change is an addition.
    pub before: Option<String>,
    /// The rendered new value, absent when the change is a removal.
    pub after: Option<String>,
}

/// The heading as the text report prints it: `placement changed`.
pub fn heading_text(heading: SystemHeading) -> &'static str {
    match heading {
        SystemHeading::PlacementChanged => "placement changed",
        SystemHeading::CompositionChanged => "composition changed",
    }
}

/// Compares two lowered systems: the composition changes first, in the new
/// closure's order and then the removed components, and then the placement
/// changes, deployment by deployment in the new system's order and then the
/// removed deployments.
pub fn diff_systems(old: &System, new: &System) -> Vec<SystemChange> {
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for component in &new.components {
        let name = component.qualified_name();
        match old
            .components
            .iter()
            .find(|before| before.qualified_name() == name)
        {
            Some(before) => compare_components(before, component, &mut changes),
            None => composition(&mut changes, name, None, Some("in the closure")),
        }
    }
    for component in &old.components {
        let name = component.qualified_name();
        if !new
            .components
            .iter()
            .any(|after| after.qualified_name() == name)
        {
            composition(&mut changes, name, Some("in the closure"), None);
        }
    }
    for deployment in &new.deployments {
        match old
            .deployments
            .iter()
            .find(|before| before.name == deployment.name)
        {
            Some(before) => compare_deployments(before, deployment, &mut changes),
            None => placement(
                &mut changes,
                deployment.name.clone(),
                None,
                Some("deployment"),
            ),
        }
    }
    for deployment in &old.deployments {
        if !new
            .deployments
            .iter()
            .any(|after| after.name == deployment.name)
        {
            placement(
                &mut changes,
                deployment.name.clone(),
                Some("deployment"),
                None,
            );
        }
    }
    changes
}

/// A component in both closures: its `offers` and `requires` lines, its
/// instances and its `external` flag.
fn compare_components(old: &Component, new: &Component, changes: &mut Vec<SystemChange>) {
    let name = new.qualified_name();
    let old_offers: Vec<&str> = old
        .offers
        .iter()
        .map(|offer| offer.service.as_str())
        .collect();
    let new_offers: Vec<&str> = new
        .offers
        .iter()
        .map(|offer| offer.service.as_str())
        .collect();
    lines(changes, &name, "offers", &old_offers, &new_offers);
    let old_requires: Vec<String> = old
        .requires
        .iter()
        .map(|require| interface_text(require.interface.as_ref()))
        .collect();
    let new_requires: Vec<String> = new
        .requires
        .iter()
        .map(|require| interface_text(require.interface.as_ref()))
        .collect();
    let old_requires: Vec<&str> = old_requires.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    let new_requires: Vec<&str> = new_requires.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    lines(changes, &name, "requires", &old_requires, &new_requires);
    if old.instances != new.instances {
        changes.push(SystemChange {
            heading: SystemHeading::CompositionChanged,
            path: format!("{name}/instances"),
            before: Some(instance_list(&old.instances)),
            after: Some(instance_list(&new.instances)),
        });
    }
    if old.external != new.external {
        changes.push(SystemChange {
            heading: SystemHeading::CompositionChanged,
            path: format!("{name}/external"),
            before: Some(old.external.to_string()),
            after: Some(new.external.to_string()),
        });
    }
}

/// The lines of one keyword that one side has and the other does not.
fn lines(
    changes: &mut Vec<SystemChange>,
    component: &str,
    keyword: &str,
    old: &[&str],
    new: &[&str],
) {
    for added in new.iter().filter(|line| !old.contains(line)) {
        composition(
            changes,
            format!("{component}/{keyword}/{added}"),
            None,
            Some(&format!("{keyword} {added}")),
        );
    }
    for removed in old.iter().filter(|line| !new.contains(line)) {
        composition(
            changes,
            format!("{component}/{keyword}/{removed}"),
            Some(&format!("{keyword} {removed}")),
            None,
        );
    }
}

/// A deployment in both systems: its machines, their `external` flags, and
/// the machine of every instance placed on both sides.
fn compare_deployments(old: &Deployment, new: &Deployment, changes: &mut Vec<SystemChange>) {
    for machine in &new.machines {
        let path = format!("{}/{}", new.name, machine.name);
        match old
            .machines
            .iter()
            .find(|before| before.name == machine.name)
        {
            Some(before) if before.external != machine.external => changes.push(SystemChange {
                heading: SystemHeading::PlacementChanged,
                path: format!("{path}/external"),
                before: Some(before.external.to_string()),
                after: Some(machine.external.to_string()),
            }),
            Some(_) => {}
            None => placement(changes, path, None, Some("machine")),
        }
    }
    for machine in &old.machines {
        if !new.machines.iter().any(|after| after.name == machine.name) {
            placement(
                changes,
                format!("{}/{}", new.name, machine.name),
                Some("machine"),
                None,
            );
        }
    }
    for placed in &new.placements {
        let moved_from = old.placements.iter().find(|before| {
            before.component == placed.component
                && before.instance == placed.instance
                && before.machine != placed.machine
        });
        if let Some(before) = moved_from {
            changes.push(SystemChange {
                heading: SystemHeading::PlacementChanged,
                path: format!("{}/{}.{}", new.name, placed.component, placed.instance),
                before: Some(before.machine.clone()),
                after: Some(placed.machine.clone()),
            });
        }
    }
}

fn composition(
    changes: &mut Vec<SystemChange>,
    path: String,
    before: Option<&str>,
    after: Option<&str>,
) {
    changes.push(SystemChange {
        heading: SystemHeading::CompositionChanged,
        path,
        before: before.map(str::to_string),
        after: after.map(str::to_string),
    });
}

fn placement(
    changes: &mut Vec<SystemChange>,
    path: String,
    before: Option<&str>,
    after: Option<&str>,
) {
    changes.push(SystemChange {
        heading: SystemHeading::PlacementChanged,
        path,
        before: before.map(str::to_string),
        after: after.map(str::to_string),
    });
}

/// `catalog.Name` for a declared interface, the service's dotted name for an
/// inline shape.
fn interface_text(interface: Option<&InterfaceRef>) -> String {
    match interface {
        Some(interface) if interface.inline => interface.name.clone(),
        Some(interface) => format!("{}.{}", interface.catalog, interface.name),
        None => String::new(),
    }
}

/// `(primary, backup)`, as `instances` is written.
fn instance_list(instances: &[String]) -> String {
    format!("({})", instances.join(", "))
}
```

```diff
+/// Compares two workspaces at the system (rsdl reference §14): the packages by
+/// the ridl categories, exactly as [`diff_sets`] does, and — when both sides
+/// carry a lowered system — the system's placement and composition changes,
+/// which carry no verdict. A system on one side only is not compared: there is
+/// nothing to compare it against.
+pub fn diff_workspaces(
+    old: &[Package],
+    old_system: Option<&System>,
+    new: &[Package],
+    new_system: Option<&System>,
+) -> DiffReport {
+    let mut report = diff_sets(old, new);
+    if let (Some(old_system), Some(new_system)) = (old_system, new_system) {
+        report.system = diff_systems(old_system, new_system);
+    }
+    report

@@ -441,4 +471,29 @@ impl serde::Serialize for Change {
 
+/// The rendered `before -> after` tail of one change line, shared by contract
+/// changes and system changes.
+fn push_values(out: &mut String, before: Option<&String>, after: Option<&String>) {
+    match (before, after) {
+        (Some(before), Some(after)) => {
+            out.push_str(": ");
+            out.push_str(before);
+            out.push_str(" -> ");
+            out.push_str(after);
+        }
+        (Some(before), None) => {
+            out.push_str(": ");
+            out.push_str(before);
+            out.push_str(" -> (removed)");

+        (None, Some(after)) => {
```

+contract changes, under two headings and with no verdict (rsdl reference §14):
+**placement changed** for a deployment or a machine added or removed, a machine
+made `external`, or an instance moved to another machine; **composition
+changed** for a component added to or removed from the system, an `offers` or
+`requires` line added or removed, `instances` changed, or a component made
+`external`. The verdict and the exit code are the contracts' alone. Moving

- Desk/veh.demo.Panel.Unit: Front -> Rear +```

+only when they hold a change. An `.ir.json` snapshot, or a directory of them,
+carries no system — `ridl baseline` publishes package snapshots only — so a

### Task 8: The book chapter and `rsdl` fences in the book

**Model:** Opus. **Story:** E6.19. **Spec:** rsdl reference §3 to §14 and
Appendix A (what the chapter teaches); plan decision P-B3; AGENTS.md "The book
describes the system as built" and "Every `ridl`/`typl` fenced block in
`docs/book/` is compiled".

This task teaches the book-example harness to compile `rsdl` fences, adds the
chapter `docs/book/rsdl.md`, written as built, and changes every sentence in the
book, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `README.md` that says rsdl is not
built.

**Decisions in this task.**

- **The harness learns one language word, nothing else.** An `rsdl` fence is
  staged as `<stem>-L<line>.rsdl` in its package's directory of the one book
  workspace — `stage` already names the file by the language word — so it names
  services and interfaces of `ridl` fences in any chapter. The import check
  learns the four rsdl declaration keywords whose names sit in the package
  namespace (rsdl §3), so an `rsdl` fence can import a component.
- **Exactly one `system` fence in the book (P-B3), enforced by the compiler.** A
  second `system` anywhere in the book is RSDL-601, which fails the run like any
  unallowed diagnostic; the harness adds no rule of its own. A test pins that
  behaviour, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `AGENTS.md` state it.
- **The chapter's packages are `veh.climate` (ridl) and `veh.cabin` (rsdl).**
  Both are new book-wide names, and they are siblings, not nested: the loader
  walks a package directory recursively, so a package nested in another's
  directory would be read twice.
- **The chapter's example allows RSDL-409 and RSDL-804 where it draws them,**
  because both are the point of the prose beside them: a redundant provider set
  is reported not yet realizable, and a backend key no backend claims is carried
  with a warning.
- **The chapter describes the built toolchain only.** It says there is no
  runtime, and every fact it states about the lowered system and `ridl diff` is
  one the tests of Tasks 2 to 7 pin or the commands in Step 6 show.
- **Other prose that says rsdl is not built changes with it:** the
  introduction's "What is built" (it also gives rsdl's version as v0.1.0; the
  reference is v0.2.0), the getting-started status table and its closing
  sentence on services, the reading guide, `README.md`'s status paragraph, the
  notice of `docs/book/reference/rsdl.md`, and `AGENTS.md`'s crate paragraph and
  book-fence convention. The `AGENTS.md` family block states no build status and
  stays as it is.

**Files:**

- Create: `docs/book/rsdl.md`
- Modify: `crates/ridl/tests/book_examples.rs` (the module comment, lines 14-17,
  29-30 and 78; `is_example_language`, line 273; `Example::language`, line 285;
  `Example::declarations`, lines 308-318; `classify`, lines 456-460;
  `verify_book`, line 780; `a_near_miss_language_word_is_refused`, lines
  1134-1153; two new tests before `markdown_in_a_subdirectory_is_read`)
- Modify: `docs/book/SUMMARY.md`, `docs/book/introduction.md` (lines 36-49 and
  91-92), `docs/book/getting-started.md` (lines 61-64 and 875-876),
  `docs/book/reading-guide.md` (lines 10-12 and 27),
  `docs/book/reference/rsdl.md` (lines 1-5), `README.md` (lines 171-173),
  `AGENTS.md` (lines 15-17 and 182-187), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (lines 59-60, 101 and
  after 109)
- Test: `crates/ridl/tests/book_examples.rs`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: the rsdl parser and checker (B3), `ridl check` over a workspace, the
  lowering and `ridl build --emit ir-json` (Tasks 2 to 6), `ridl diff` at the
  system (Task 7).
- Produces: `rsdl` as a verified fence language in `docs/book/`; the chapter
  `docs/book/rsdl.md` in the book's table of contents after Getting started.

+/// An `rsdl` fence is verified: it is staged as an `.rsdl` file in the one book

+#[test]

+    let contract = "```ridl\npackage zz.contract\n\ntype Level : integer [0..7]\n\n\

+                    service zz.contract.fan : Fan\n```\n\n";

+        ),

+    assert!(
+        report.contains("RSDL-310"),
+        "the rsdl block must be compiled, got:\n{report}"
+    );

+         ```rsdl\npackage zz.two\n\nsystem Two {}\n```\n",
+    );

+    assert!(
+        report.contains("RSDL-601"),
+        "the second system must draw RSDL-601, got:\n{report}"
+    );
+}

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the chapter**

Create `docs/book/rsdl.md`, titled `# Describing a system`, with these sections
in order. Wrap prose at 80 columns by hand: `docs/book/` is in `.primignore`.
Each fence below is compiled by the harness and is written exactly as given.

1. **Opening** (no heading): the getting-started chapter declares contracts,
   this one declares what uses them; rsdl is the architecture layer; link
   `reference/rsdl.md`. What is built: the compiler reads `.rsdl` files, checks
   every rule of the reference, lowers the checked system to the IR beside the
   package IR, and `ridl diff` compares two systems. What is not built: no
   runtime reads the lowered system; the links, routing table and grants are
   facts the compiler writes down, and nothing delivers a message along them.
2. **`## Contracts first`**: rsdl declares no type, interface or service (an
   `.rsdl` file that tries is RSDL-604); the contracts are one ridl package;
   after the fence, `veh.climate.diag` has an inline shape, so its dotted name
   is the name of its one interface.

```ridl
package veh.climate

type Temperature : integer [-40..85]
type FanLevel : integer [0..7]
type Heated : boolean

interface Climate {
  signal cabinTemperature : Temperature @[100ms..1s]
  command setFan(level : FanLevel) @[..50ms]
}

interface Seats {
  signal heated : Heated @[100ms..1s]
}

service veh.climate.control : Climate
service veh.climate.seats : Seats

service veh.climate.diag {
  query readTemperature() : Temperature @[..100ms]
}
```

3. **`## Components`**: a component is one execution context, offers services,
   requires interfaces, one reference per line. After the fence, a list:
   `requires` names an interface, never a provider (the compiler finds the one
   service that lists it and the one component that offers it); `instances`
   names the copies, and a component without it has one instance `Unit` in the
   IR and in diagnostics; `external` marks no implementation here;
   `ClimateControl` has two instances, a redundant provider set, so
   `Dashboard`'s and `PhoneApp`'s `requires Climate` each draw RSDL-409, "not
   yet realizable", which the fence allows for that reason.

```rsdl,allow=RSDL-409
package veh.cabin

import veh.climate.Climate
import veh.climate.Seats

/// Runs the climate loop. Two copies, one per zone controller.
component ClimateControl [ instances = (front, rear) ] {
  offers   veh.climate.control
  requires Seats
}

component SeatHeating { offers veh.climate.seats }

component Dashboard {
  requires Climate
  requires Seats
}

/// The phone app. No implementation in this workspace.
component PhoneApp [ external ] {
  requires Climate
  requires veh.climate.diag
}
```

4. **`## The system`**: a `system` is the closure; a workspace declares at most
   one. After the fence: `veh.climate.diag` is a lone service no declared
   component offers, so it stands for an implicit component of that name with
   one `Unit` instance.

```rsdl
package veh.cabin

system Cabin { ClimateControl, SeatHeating, Dashboard, PhoneApp, veh.climate.diag }
```

5. **`## Distributions`**: what is versioned, signed and shipped together; when
   a workspace declares one, every implemented closure component is in exactly
   one. After the fence: `PhoneApp` is external and in none; `Display` depends
   on `Comfort` because `Dashboard` requires interfaces `ClimateControl` and
   `SeatHeating` offer; the reverse would be a tier inversion, RSDL-901.

```rsdl
package veh.cabin

distribution Comfort [ tier = PLATFORM ] { ClimateControl, SeatHeating, veh.climate.diag }
distribution Display [ tier = APPLICATION ] { Dashboard }
```

6. **`## Deployments and machines`**: a deployment places the closure; machines
   list what they host; every instance on exactly one machine per deployment.
   After the fence, a list: a bare component name places every instance,
   `ClimateControl.front` one; an `external` machine hosts external components
   only, and on `Rig` `PhoneApp` is a stub on an on-board machine;
   `linux.cpuset` is a backend key carried uninterpreted on `Dashboard`'s
   placement, and no configured backend claims `linux`, so RSDL-804. Then, in
   prose with no fence: writing `ClimateControl` in place of
   `ClimateControl.front` on `ZoneFront` reaches `ClimateControl.rear` twice in
   `Car` (RSDL-706), and dropping the `ZoneRear` machine leaves
   `ClimateControl.rear` on no machine (RSDL-701); each blocks only its own
   deployment.

```rsdl,allow=RSDL-804
package veh.cabin

deployment Car for Cabin {
  machine ZoneFront [ labels = (ASIL_A) ] { ClimateControl.front, SeatHeating, veh.climate.diag }
  machine ZoneRear { ClimateControl.rear }
  machine Head { Dashboard [ linux.cpuset = (1) ] }
  machine Cloud [ external ] { PhoneApp }
}

deployment Bench for Cabin {
  machine Rig { ClimateControl, SeatHeating, Dashboard, veh.climate.diag, PhoneApp }
}
```

7. **`## What the compiler writes`**: `ridl check` reports every rule above;
   `ridl build --emit ir-json` writes the package IR and the lowered system,
   shown as a `sh` fence `ridl build --emit ir-json --out-dir out && ls out` and
   a `text` fence listing `veh.cabin.Cabin.system.json`, `veh.cabin.ir.json`,
   `veh.climate.ir.json`; `ir-text` and `ir-binary` write `.system.txtpb` and
   `.system.binpb`. Then a list of the facts, once for the closure and once per
   deployment: components, producers (marked not yet realizable with more than
   one instance) and grants (`PhoneApp` reaches `veh.climate`, lowered with the
   `external` flag); the region map with each interface's number from the
   package's lock; placements and links with crossing kinds (in `Car`,
   `ClimateControl.front → SeatHeating.Unit` same machine,
   `ClimateControl.rear → SeatHeating.Unit` different machine, every link from
   `PhoneApp` off-board; in `Bench` every link same machine); the routing table;
   the surface set (the three links from `PhoneApp`, the external side
   consuming, in both deployments, because the flag and not the machine defines
   the boundary); installation (in `Car`, `Comfort` on `ZoneFront` and
   `ZoneRear`, `Display` on `Head`) and dependency. Close with the gating: a
   closure error writes nothing; an RSDL-7xx error leaves its deployment out and
   the build exits 1.
8. **`## Comparing two systems`**: contracts by the ridl categories; system
   changes under two headings with no verdict. Moving `Dashboard` from `Head` to
   `ZoneRear` in `Car` gives this `text` fence:

   ```text
   identical
   placement changed
     Car/veh.cabin.Dashboard.Unit: Head -> ZoneRear
   ```

   Then: a `requires` line added, or a component added to the system, is listed
   under `composition changed`; which of these changes break a deployed system
   is not decided by the compiler.
9. **`## What is not here yet`**, a list: no runtime reads the lowered system
   and no descriptor is emitted from it; rsdl declares no transport, network or
   process; posture, redundancy arbitration and the `optional` requirement are
   reserved (rsdl reference §12).

Run: `cargo test -p ridl --test book_examples book_examples_compile`

Expected: PASS. A stale allowance fails the run, so the PASS also shows that the
components fence draws RSDL-409 and the deployments fence RSDL-804.

### Task 9: The catalog hash in each region, and gardening lane B's plan

**Model:** Fable. **Story:** E6.17. **Spec:** rsdl reference §13 ("the catalog
hash per catalog, computed by ridl … embedded here as an input, never computed
by rsdl"; "The catalog hashes of every catalog in the region map, as received");
plan decision P-B2; catalog descriptor plan Task 4
(`ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash`); lock design §11 (Task 4 and Task 3's
follow-up edits after L4).

This task fills the hash of every region of the lowered system with the hash the
catalog descriptor carries for that catalog, and then gardens lane B's plan. It
is the last task of lane B.

**Inputs from other lanes, re-checked when this task starts.** The hash comes
from #324, with the signatures of the catalog descriptor plan (Tasks 3 and 4):
`ridl_descriptor::number::{Numbered, number_interfaces(package: &Package) -> Vec<Numbered>}`
and
`ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash(package: &Package, others: &[&Package], numbered: &[Numbered]) -> [u8; 32]`,
with `ridlc` depending on `ridl-descriptor` (#324 Task 9 adds the `catalog`
emit). The lock design §11 changes both functions after L4: `number_interfaces`
reads the numbers from the IR, `Numbered` tells an inline shape from an
interface spelled the same, and the hash takes the interfaces in number order.
Step 1 checks the names as #324 merged them; if either signature differs, use
the merged one in `lower_workspace_system` and in the test, and report the
difference on #328.

**When this task runs.** P-B2: after #324's Task 4 has merged. If B4's Tasks 1
to 8 are already merged or in review, this task is a second pull request on its
own branch from `main`, and Step 7 runs in that pull request, because it is the
one that completes lane B.

**Decisions in this task.**

- **The driver embeds the hash; the rsdl lowering does not compute it.** rsdl
  §13 makes the hash an input. `ridl_sem::rsdl::lower_system` leaves
  `Region.hash` empty and does not depend on `ridl-descriptor`; `ridlc` gains
  `lower_workspace_system`, which lowers and then sets each region's hash from
  `catalog_hash`. `run_build`, `compile_workspace` (and so `ridl diff`) and the
  corpus runner call it, so every lowered system the toolchain returns or writes
  carries its hashes.
- **The hash is the catalog descriptor's.** `lower_workspace_system` calls
  `catalog_hash` with the catalog's package, every other workspace package and
  `ridl.std`, and `number_interfaces` of the package. Before committing, read
  how #324's `ridlc` computes the descriptor's hash for `--emit catalog`
  (`write_emits`, the `Emit::Catalog` arm); the `others` list must be the same
  set of packages, or the two hashes differ for a catalog whose types reach
  `ridl.std`. If #324 passes a different list, pass that list here too.
- **`bytes hash = 3` on `Region`.** 32 bytes when set; canonical JSON renders it
  as base64 (ADR-0014 decision 14).

**Files:**

- Modify: `crates/ridl-ir/proto/ridl/ir/v2/system.proto` (`Region`)
- Modify: `crates/ridl-ir/src/lib.rs` (the `system_round_trip` fixture's region
  and the JSON test)
- Modify: `crates/ridl-sem/src/rsdl/lower.rs` (`regions`)
- Modify: `crates/ridlc/src/lib.rs` (`compile_workspace`; `run_build`'s system
  write; a new `lower_workspace_system` before `blocks_every_artifact`)
- Modify: `crates/ridlc/tests/corpus.rs` (`compile_entry`'s `system_json`),
  `crates/ridlc/tests/snapshots/corpus__system@rsdl-appendix-a.snap`,
  `crates/ridlc/tests/corpus/rsdl-appendix-a/NOTES`
- Modify: `docs/book/rsdl.md` (the region map item)
- Test: `crates/ridlc/tests/cli.rs` (before `placement_workspace`)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ridl_descriptor::number::number_interfaces`,
  `ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash` (#324); `ridl_sem::lower_system` (Task
  4), `ridlc::WorkspaceOutput::{checked, std_ir, system}` (Task 6).
- Produces:
  - `Region.hash: Vec<u8>` (`bytes hash = 3`)
  - `pub fn ridlc::lower_workspace_system(system: &CheckedSystem, packages: &[&ridl_ir::v2::Package], std_ir: &ridl_ir::v2::Package) -> Option<ridl_ir::v2::System>`

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test**

In `crates/ridlc/tests/cli.rs`, insert before the doc comment of
`placement_workspace`:

```rust
/// rsdl reference §13 and roadmap E6.17: each region of the lowered system
/// carries the catalog hash of its catalog, 32 bytes, as
/// `ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash` computes it from the package IR; the
/// build writes the same system `compile_workspace` returns.
#[test]
fn each_region_carries_its_catalog_hash() {
    let entry = Path::new("tests/corpus/rsdl-appendix-a");
    let mut db = ridl_core::RidlDatabase::default();
    let output = ridlc::compile_workspace(&mut db, entry).expect("the corpus entry loads");
    let system = output.system.expect("Appendix A lowers");
    let packages: Vec<&ridl_ir::v2::Package> =
        output.checked.iter().map(|package| &package.ir).collect();
    let catalogs: Vec<&str> = system
        .regions
        .iter()
        .map(|region| region.catalog.as_str())
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(catalogs, ["veh.adas", "veh.diag"]);
    for region in &system.regions {
        let package = packages
            .iter()
            .find(|package| package.name == region.catalog)
            .expect("a region names a workspace package");
        let others: Vec<&ridl_ir::v2::Package> = packages
            .iter()
            .copied()
            .filter(|other| other.name != package.name)
            .chain(std::iter::once(&output.std_ir))
            .collect();
        let expected = ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash(
            package,
            &others,
            &ridl_descriptor::number::number_interfaces(package),
        );
        assert_eq!(region.hash, expected, "`{}`", region.catalog);
    }
    assert_ne!(system.regions[0].hash, system.regions[1].hash);

    let out = TempDir::new("system-hash-out");
    let (code, stderr) = ridlc(&[
        "build".as_ref(),
        entry.as_os_str(),
        "--out-dir".as_ref(),
        out.path().as_os_str(),
        "--emit".as_ref(),
        "ir-json".as_ref(),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "stderr:\n{stderr}");
    let json = std::fs::read_to_string(out.path().join("veh.topology.Vehicle.system.json"))
        .expect("the system is written");
    assert_eq!(
        ridl_ir::v2::system_from_json(&json).expect("the JSON artifact parses"),
        system
    );
}
```

```diff
+/// Lowers the workspace's system (rsdl reference §13) and embeds in each region
+/// the catalog hash of its catalog, as `ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash`
+/// computes it for the catalog descriptor: over the catalog's interfaces, their
+/// numbers and every type they reach. The hash is an input to the rsdl
+/// lowering, which never computes it, so the driver fills it in.
+///
+/// `packages` is every package of the workspace, and `std_ir` the lowered
+/// `ridl.std`, which a reached type may name. `None` when
+/// [`lower_system`] is.
+pub fn lower_workspace_system(
+    system: &CheckedSystem,
+    packages: &[&ridl_ir::v2::Package],
+    std_ir: &ridl_ir::v2::Package,
+) -> Option<ridl_ir::v2::System> {
+    let mut lowered = lower_system(system, packages)?;
+    for region in &mut lowered.regions {
+        let package = packages
+            .iter()
+            .find(|package| package.name == region.catalog)
+            .expect("a region's catalog is a package of the workspace");
+        let others: Vec<&ridl_ir::v2::Package> = packages
+            .iter()
+            .copied()
+            .filter(|other| other.name != package.name)
+            .chain(std::iter::once(std_ir))
+            .collect();
+        let numbered = ridl_descriptor::number::number_interfaces(package);
+        region.hash = ridl_descriptor::hash::catalog_hash(package, &others, &numbered).to_vec();
+    }
+    Some(lowered)
+}
+

 /// except an RSDL-7xx one, which blocks only the lowering of its own
```
